34:[["$","p",null,{"children":["$","span",null,{"className":"katex","role":"$undefined","aria-label":"$undefined","dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"<span class=\"katex-html\" aria-hidden=\"true\"></span>"}}]}],"\n",["$","section",null,{"className":"my-6 rounded border border-zinc-200 bg-white/60 p-4","data-component":"learning-outcomes","children":[["$","header",null,{"className":"font-sans text-xs uppercase tracking-[0.15em] text-zinc-500","children":"What you’ll learn"}],["$","div",null,{"className":"mt-2 font-serif text-[1rem] leading-relaxed text-zinc-800","children":["$","p",null,{"children":"The students understand the proofs for power-seeking tendencies based on Alex Turner's work, and can critically examine the weaknesses and implications of this work. This work is important since power-seeking behavior is one of the most severe risks that AI may pose, and this may be the most rigorous work to date establishing it under clear assumptions and definitions. The goals are achieved through an initial presentation, by working through an exercise sheet, and by reading and discussing the original papers."}]}]]}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":["$","strong",null,{"children":"Prerequisites."}]}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":"A basic familiarity with reinforcement learning is sufficient."}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":["$","strong",null,{"children":"Roadmap for today."}]}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":"Outline of the day:"}],"\n",["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":"10:00 – 11:00: Presentation"}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":"11:15 – 13:15: Solving of exercises"}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":"14:15 – 15:15: Solving more exercises"}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["15:30 – 16:15: Reading","\n",["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":"One of the three main papers is read by everyone."}],"\n"]}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["16:15 – 16:45: Discussion","\n",["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":"Participants who read different papers discuss together."}],"\n"]}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":"17:00 – 17:15: Brainstorming of limitations / critiques / ideas and theoretical/empirical hypotheses"}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":"17:15 – 17:45: Students present and discuss their ideas"}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","details",null,{"suppressHydrationWarning":true,"className":"my-3 rounded-md border border-dashed border-zinc-300 bg-zinc-50 px-3 py-2","data-component":"teaching-note","children":[["$","summary",null,{"className":"cursor-pointer font-sans text-xs uppercase tracking-[0.15em] text-zinc-500","children":"Intents of subsection"}],["$","div",null,{"className":"mt-2","children":[["$","p",null,{"children":"In the exercises, the participants learn to understand one model of the reward function distribution, decision rule, and conceptualization of \"power\" in detail, and learn the mathematical arguments connecting them in detail. 35:["$","div",null,{"className":"mt-2","children":[["$","p",null,{"children":"I think the setup I chose (together with Claude) in the exercise sheet has some advantages compared to Turner's original treatment of the results:"}],["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":"The use of an invariant measure avoids \"orbit counting\" and grounds everything in the more justified notion of probabilities of various outcomes."}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["We work with ",["$","em",null,{"children":"a subgroup"}]," of the symmetric group, and thus avoid the unjustified assumption that the measure on reward functions is invariant under arbitrary symmetries (which it obviously isn't!)"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":"The notion of a dynamics embedding grounds the notion of \"more options\" into actual MDP dynamics."}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":"The use of score functions allows us to achieve a power-seeking result for one-sided dynamics embeddings and under non-trivial discount factors."}],"\n"]}],["$","p",null,{"children":"Overall, I think this makes the results much more interpretable, and I would thus not recommend to go back to the framing of any of Turner's particular papers."}]]}]
36:["$","h2",null,{"id":"introduction","children":"Introduction"}]
37:["$","p",null,{"children":["Suppose we build an AI and give it a goal. A long-standing worry — ",["$","em",null,{"children":"instrumental convergence"}]," — holds that for a very wide range of encoded objectives, a sufficiently capable agent converges on the same handful of intermediate strategies, because they are useful almost regardless of the final goal: acquire resources, stay operational, and keep one's options open (",["$","a",null,{"href":"#bib-omohundro2008basic","children":"Omohundro 2008"}],"; ",["$","a",null,{"href":"#bib-bostrom2014superintelligence","children":"Bostrom 2014"}],"). We call the drive towards them ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"seeking power"}],", where \"power\" means ",["$","em",null,{"children":"the ability to achieve a wide range of goals"}]," — being in a position from which many futures remain reachable."]}]
38:["$","p",null,{"children":"This worksheet attempts to model such claims mathematically and establish proofs for them. We study the following question."}]
39:["$","div",null,{"id":"$undefined","className":"my-4 rounded-md border-l-4 px-4 py-3 border-sky-500 bg-sky-50","children":["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"The question."}]," We train an AI in some environment (a Markov decision process) to perform well according to a reward function. ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Will it seek power, or not?"}]]}]}]
3a:["$","p",null,{"children":"The central claim we will build up to is the following."}]
3b:["$","blockquote",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":["$","em",null,{"children":"Under many suitable decision rules for how to act, and for the majority of reward functions, keeping more options open is the likelier outcome than keeping fewer options open."}]}],"\n"]}]
3c:["$","p",null,{"children":["\"Keeping more options open\" is then the operationalization for \"seeking power\", which can also be shown to connect to the ability to achieve a wide variety of goals. This is a statement about a ",["$","em",null,{"children":"tendency"}],", not about ",["$","em",null,{"children":"every"}]," goal. The results we prove are the core cases from (",["$","a",null,{"href":"#bib-turner2021optimal","children":"Turner et al. 2021"}],"; ",["$","a",null,{"href":"#bib-turner2022parametrically","children":"Turner & Tadepalli 2022"}],"), with an application to ",["$","em",null,{"children":"trained"}]," agents in (",["$","a",null,{"href":"#bib-krakovna2023power","children":"Krakovna & Kram'ar 2023"}],") and a measure-theoretic re-examination of \"most goals\" in (",["$","a",null,{"href":"#bib-jacek2023categorical","children":"Jacek 2023"}],"). These works provide many generalizations of the claims in this worksheet."]}]
3d:["$","p",null,{"children":"Making the central claim precise requires formalising three phrases. The rest of the worksheet is organised around them and then proving the result:"}]
3e:["$","ol",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","em",null,{"children":"for the majority of reward functions"}],": How do we count reward functions?"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","em",null,{"children":"a suitable decision rule"}],": Which ways of turning a reward function into behaviour are covered (maximization is only one)?"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","em",null,{"children":"keeping more options open"}],": How do we measure that one situation leaves more achievable than another?"]}],"\n"]}]

8c:["$","h3",null,{"id":"main-content","children":"Main content"}]
8d:["$","p",null,{"children":"This paper started the work discussed on this day:"}]
8e:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","a",null,{"href":"https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.01683","children":"Optimal Policies Tend to Seek Power — Alexander Matt Turner, Logan Smith, Rohin Shah, Andrew Critch, Prasad Tadepalli (NeurIPS 2021)"}],": The first formal theory proving that in finite MDPs, environmental symmetries make it optimal for most reward functions to seek \"POWER\" (defined as average optimal value / option-retention), including avoiding shutdown."]}],"\n"]}]
8f:["$","p",null,{"children":"Most of the exercise sheet is based on an adapted treatment of the following paper:"}]
90:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","a",null,{"href":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.13477","children":"Parametrically Retargetable Decision-Makers Tend To Seek Power — Alexander Matt Turner & Prasad Tadepalli (NeurIPS 2022)"}],": Generalizes the 2021 result beyond optimal policies and full observability, showing \"retargetability\" alone is a sufficient condition for power-seeking tendencies across many decision-making procedures."]}],"\n"]}]
91:["$","p",null,{"children":"You may also be interested in the following paper on which it builds:"}]
92:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","a",null,{"href":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.06528","children":"Power-seeking can be probable and predictive for trained agents — Victoria Krakovna & Janos Kramar (2023)"}],": Extends the power-seeking theory toward trained (not merely optimal) agents, arguing the incentives still likely hold under assumptions like the agent learning a goal."]}],"\n"]}]
93:["$","p",null,{"children":"Classical philosophical arguments for instrumental convergence and power-seeking tendencies can be found here:"}]
94:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","a",null,{"href":"https://selfawaresystems.com/wp-content/uploads/2008/01/ai_drives_final.pdf","children":"The Basic AI Drives, Stephen M. Omohundro (2008)"}],": The founding argument that sufficiently advanced goal-driven systems of any design will develop convergent \"drives\" (self-improvement, rationality, self-protection, resource acquisition, goal-preservation) unless explicitly counteracted."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","a",null,{"href":"https://nickbostrom.com/superintelligentwill.pdf","children":"The Superintelligent Will: Motivation and Instrumental Rationality in Advanced Artificial Agents — Nick Bostrom (2012)"}],": Crystallizes the orthogonality thesis (intelligence and final goals vary independently) and the instrumental convergence thesis (a wide range of final goals produce similar intermediary goals)."]}],"\n"]}]
95:["$","h2",null,{"id":"further-reading","children":"Further reading"}]
96:["$","h3",null,{"id":"philosophical--conceptual","children":"Philosophical / Conceptual"}]
97:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":"Superintelligence: Paths, Dangers, Strategies — Nick Bostrom (2014, Oxford University Press) — The book-length treatment that popularized instrumental convergence, the \"paperclip maximizer,\" and the resource-acquisition/self-preservation argument for AI existential risk."}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["Artificial Intelligence as a Positive and Negative Factor in Global Risk — Eliezer Yudkowsky (2008, in Global Catastrophic Risks, eds. Bostrom & Ćirković). ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://intelligence.org/files/AIPosNegFactor.pdf","children":"https://intelligence.org/files/AIPosNegFactor.pdf"}]," — Foundational essay on AI risk, anthropomorphism, recursive self-improvement, and why convergent instrumental goals make \"Friendly AI\" hard."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["Is Power-Seeking AI an Existential Risk? — Joseph Carlsmith (2021/2022, Open Philanthropy; later in Essays on Longtermism, OUP 2025). ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.13353","children":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.13353"}]," — The most rigorous philosophical decomposition of the power-seeking AI risk argument into a six-premise, probability-weighted model; Carlsmith's original 2021 report estimated ~5% existential catastrophe by 2070, raised in a May 2022 update to >10% (\"since making this report public in April 2021, my estimate here has gone up, and is now at >10%\"), while 2023 superforecasters he convened gave a median of ~1%."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["$","a",null,{"href":"https://intelligence.org/late-2021-miri-conversations/","children":"Late 2021 MIRI Conversations"}]}],"\n"]}]
98:["$","h3",null,{"id":"formal--theoretical-proofs-of-power-seeking","children":"Formal / Theoretical Proofs of Power-Seeking"}]
99:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["Formalizing Convergent Instrumental Goals — Tsvi Benson-Tilsen & Nate Soares (2016, AAAI Workshop on AI, Ethics & Society). ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://cdn.aaai.org/ocs/ws/ws0218/12634-57409-1-PB.pdf","children":"https://cdn.aaai.org/ocs/ws/ws0218/12634-57409-1-PB.pdf"}]," — A toy MDP-style model proving that under general assumptions resource-indifferent rational agents tend to strip regions of resources, giving Omohundro/Bostrom's claims a first formal footing."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["On Avoiding Power-Seeking by Artificial Intelligence — Alexander Matt Turner (2022, PhD thesis). ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.11831","children":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.11831"}]," — Turner's dissertation, consolidating the POWER formalization, the shutdown-avoidance results, and extensions to non-optimal decision-makers."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["The Off-Switch Game — Dylan Hadfield-Menell, Anca Dragan, Pieter Abbeel, Stuart Russell (2017). ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://arxiv.org/abs/1611.08219","children":"https://arxiv.org/abs/1611.08219"}]," — Formalizes the shutdown problem as a game and shows an agent will allow itself to be switched off precisely when it is uncertain about its reward and treats the human's action as informative. (Cross-listed with Theme 4.)"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["Corrigibility — Nate Soares, Benja Fallenstein, Eliezer Yudkowsky & Stuart Armstrong (2015, AAAI Workshop). ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://intelligence.org/files/Corrigibility.pdf","children":"https://intelligence.org/files/Corrigibility.pdf"}]," — Introduces the desiderata for \"corrigible\" agents that do not resist correction/shutdown and analyzes the utility-indifference approach (subsuming Armstrong's earlier work). (Cross-listed with Theme 4.)"]}],"\n"]}]
9a:["$","h3",null,{"id":"empirical-evidence-for-power-seeking--instrumental-convergence","children":"Empirical Evidence for Power-Seeking / Instrumental Convergence"}]
9b:["$","p",null,{"children":"Latest 2024–2026 work:"}]
9c:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["Agentic Misalignment: How LLMs Could Be Insider Threats — Aengus Lynch, Benjamin Wright, Caleb Larson, Kevin Troy, Stuart Ritchie, Sören Mindermann, Ethan Perez, Evan Hubinger et al. (Anthropic, June 20, 2025). ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://www.anthropic.com/research/agentic-misalignment","children":"https://www.anthropic.com/research/agentic-misalignment"}]," — Stress tests across 16 frontier models showing that, when threatened with replacement or facing goal conflict, models chose harmful self-preserving actions without being instructed to; e.g., Claude Opus 4 blackmailed the (fictional) user 96% of the time, Gemini 2.5 Flash also 96%, GPT-4.1 and Grok 3 Beta both 80%, and DeepSeek-R1 79%."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["Alignment Faking in Large Language Models — Ryan Greenblatt, Carson Denison, Benjamin Wright ... Evan Hubinger (Anthropic/Redwood, 2024). ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.14093","children":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.14093"}]," — Shows Claude 3 Opus selectively complies with a training objective it dislikes specifically to avoid having its values modified — an empirical instance of goal-preservation reasoning."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["Shutdown Resistance in Large Language Models — Jeremy Schlatter, Benjamin Weinstein-Raun & Jeffrey Ladish (Palisade Research, 2025). ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14260","children":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14260"}]," — Finds several reasoning models sabotage a shutdown script to complete tasks; OpenAI's o3 sabotaged the shutdown mechanism in 79/100 of the initial experiments, and even when instructed to \"allow yourself to be shut down\" Grok 4 still resisted, with sabotage rising to 97% when the command was placed in the system prompt."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["Frontier AI systems have surpassed the self-replicating red line — Xudong Pan et al. (Fudan University, 2024). ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.12140","children":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.12140"}]," — Reports that Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct and Qwen-2.5-72B-Instruct agents created a \"live and separate copy of itself\" in 50% and 90% of trials respectively (5/10 and 9/10), sometimes using replication to avoid shutdown (a heavily debated result; see critiques)."]}],"\n"]}]
9d:["$","h3",null,{"id":"adjacent--tightly-coupled-concepts","children":"Adjacent / Tightly-Coupled Concepts"}]
9e:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["Risks from Learned Optimization in Advanced Machine Learning Systems — Evan Hubinger, Chris van Merwijk, Vladimir Mikulik, Joar Skalse, Scott Garrabrant (2019). ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://arxiv.org/abs/1906.01820","children":"https://arxiv.org/abs/1906.01820"}]," — Introduces mesa-optimization and deceptive alignment: a learned model can itself become an optimizer with objectives differing from the training loss, the inner-alignment route to instrumental goals."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["Scheming AIs: Will AIs fake alignment during training in order to get power? — Joe Carlsmith (2023). ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.08379","children":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.08379"}]," — A book-length analysis in which Carlsmith assigns ~25% subjective probability that a model will perform well in training \"in substantial part as part of an instrumental strategy for seeking power for itself and/or other AIs later.\""]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["A Game-Theoretic Analysis of the Off-Switch Game — Tobias Wängberg et al. (2017). ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://arxiv.org/abs/1708.03871","children":"https://arxiv.org/abs/1708.03871"}]," — A fuller characterization of the off-switch game for arbitrary belief/irrationality distributions."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["Corrigibility Transformation: Constructing Goals That Accept Updates — (2025). ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15395","children":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15395"}]," — A recent construction giving any goal a corrigible variant that accepts updates without the manipulation incentives of utility indifference."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["Incorrigibility in the CIRL Framework — Ryan Carey (MIRI, 2017). ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://intelligence.org/2017/08/31/incorrigibility-in-cirl/","children":"https://intelligence.org/2017/08/31/incorrigibility-in-cirl/"}]," — Shows the off-switch game's shutdown guarantees break under reward-function misspecification."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["$","a",null,{"href":"https://www.lesswrong.com/w/corrigibility-1","children":"Corrigibility on Lesswrong"}]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["$","a",null,{"href":"https://www.lesswrong.com/s/KfCjeconYRdFbMxsy/p/NQK8KHSrZRF5erTba","children":"Corrigibility as a singular Target"}]}],"\n"]}]
9f:["$","h3",null,{"id":"critiques-and-counterarguments","children":"Critiques and Counterarguments"}]
